Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #1
16 May 2022
PRESENT
Mayor Eddie Ellington, Presiding; and Council Members Marion B. Williams, Sara M. Coffey,
D. Michael Rainey, Melissa Elliott, Garry D. Daeke, Ola Thorpe-Cooper, and Jason A. Spriggs
ABSENT
Council Member William Burnette
STAFF PRESENT
City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon, Finance Director Joey Fuqua, City Clerk Esther J.
McCrackin, Assistant City Manager Paylor Spruill, Kerr Lake Regional Water and Henderson
Water Reclamation Director Christy Lipscomb, Public Works Director Andy Perkinson and
Assistant Public Works Director Lee Owens, Customer Service Manager Shay Bennett, Fire
Chief Steve Cordell, and Assistant Fire Chief Curtis Tyndall
CALL TO ORDER
The 16 May 2022 Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #1 was called to order by
Mayor Eddie Ellington at 6:03 p.m. in the R. G. “Chick” Young, Jr. Council Chambers,
Municipal Building, 134 Rose Avenue, Henderson, NC.
ROLL CALL
The City Clerk called the roll and advised Mayor Ellington a quorum was present.
OPENING REMARKS
Mayor Ellington welcomed the staff members in attendance and then turned the meeting over to
City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon.

Minute Book 45
16 May 2022 Budget Work Session #1 Minutes
Page 1 of 6

Manager Blackmon began by saying he hoped Council had taken the time to review the budget
material distributed during the May 9th meeting and he said he is looking forward to Council’s
requests and responding to questions. Ultimately, the Manager would like to have a consensus
for a fair and well received budget.
Mr. Blackmon then turned the discussion over to Finance Director Joey Fuqua.
Kerr Lake Regional Water (KLRW)
Mr. Fuqua began by recapping this fund which is very consistent from year to year. He said the
FY22 budget was $5,208,000 and the FY23 proposed budget if $5,312,490 which is a 2.01%
increase. He stated the Capital Outlay requests centered around a pump and lab equipment.
Utility costs are volatile due to inflation and fuel costs, and chemicals are the biggest increase in
this budget. Mr. Fuqua said the FY23 proposed budget includes these unknowns. The Debt
service for KLRW includes one high speed pump.
Council Member Spriggs asked if chemical costs are stable. KLRW Director Christy Lipscomb
said the costs are up 3.5% currently and she has received a notice that the disinfectant (bleach)
used at the plant will be harder to obtain so her department is currently making plans
accordingly.
Council Member Daeke asked about the largest expenditure in the Capital Reserves Fund. Mr.
Fuqua said this relates to the 20mgd reserve.
Council Member Williams expressed concern as to whether enough has been added to the budget
for unforeseen chemical increases. Mr. Fuqua felt the budget includes enough to cover the
unknowns.
Council Member Coffey asked about staffing and Council Member Williams asked about
salaries not meeting or exceeding what other municipalities are offering. The City Manager said
he would like to discuss staffing/salary issues during the General Fund discussion.
Council Member Elliott asked about coverage for retirees and again the Manager said he would
like to discuss this during the General Fund portion of the Work Sessions.
Manager Blackmon did, however, mention that there are some departments where he would like
to make salary adjustments. He mentioned the possibility of using bonuses to entice individuals
to stay with the City and gave an example of increasing salaries by $1.00 as this will increase a
paycheck more than increasing pay by a percentage of salary.
Council Member Daeke then turned the discussion to public education. Ms. Lipscomb said they
are working on compiling brochures for issues such as FOG (fats, oil and grease) to use at
special events such as Night Out Against Crime, Halloween, etc. Once the brochures are
completed, she said she will share them with Council.

Minute Book 45
16 May 2022 Budget Work Session #1 Minutes
Page 2 of 6

Council Member Spriggs expressed concern about the vacant position at HWRF. City Manager
Blackmon said this position is funded and until someone is found for the position, it will be filled
by Interim Director Christy Lipscomb. He said Ms. Lipscomb has done a good job of training
staff. Mr. Spriggs said he wants to make sure Ms. Lipscomb is not overworked as he would not
want to have her leave the City.
With no other discussion, Mayor Ellington asked Council for a consensus on the recommended
budget for KLRW of $5,312,490. The overwhelming consensus was to approve this
recommendation.
Mr. Fuqua then moved the discussion to the Water Fund.
Water Fund
Mr. Fuqua recapped the Water Fund by saying the proposed budget of $7,530,571 is very similar
to FY22 as there is a -0.73% decrease due to a reduction in capital outlay. Notable items in this
Fund were repairs, water taps, meter replacements and equipment. The new HVAC cost is
shared with other departments as is a new ice machine. Mr. Fuqua said debt service has been
consistent over the last few years with no notable issues.
Council Member Coffey asked about the engineering increases. The Manager said contracted
services have increased slightly.
Council Member Daeke asked what percentage of water lines have been replaced and how many
more need to be replaced. Public Works Director Andy Perkinson said they have mainly focused
on responding to breaks. At this time there is no prioritization of what lines need to be replaced.
City Manager Blackmon said he is working on an RFP to contract an engineer. He added a grant
was just submitted for a stormwater grant.
Public Works Director Andy Perkinson said a lot of lines need to be taken out of service and
there was a brief discussion about specific street issues regarding these smaller lines.
Manager Blackmon said many of these problems are due to the lack of stormwater planning. He
said a plan has been submitted to Raleigh and once it is approved, the City will have five (5)
years to comply. Ms. Lipscomb added that approval is anticipated in August and that there is a
very specific timeline that will need to be met within the compliance period. She said she is
currently working on a portion of the plan to get a jump on the requirements. When the plan is
approved, she said it will include other departments such as Police and Fire.
Council Member Williams asked about the water problems on personal property. The Manager
said the City is doing all it can to prevent damage; however, it cannot do work on personal
property.

Minute Book 45
16 May 2022 Budget Work Session #1 Minutes
Page 3 of 6

This led to a brief discussion about how the lack of planning and development is causing water
problems, soil erosion, etc. There was mention of a problem on school property and Ms.
Lipscomb stated anything to do with federal or state funds such as a school should be addressed
by the State.
Ms. Williams then asked about the radio read meters. Water Collections Manager Shay Bennett
said about 60% of the meters are radio read. She added there are funds in the proposed budget
for more radio read meters.
Council Member Daeke remembered that when the City moved to radio read meters it would
allow for a reduction in staff. He asked if this has happened. Ms. Bennett said the department
utilizes one less meter reader than before this project began. She said there are currently three
full-time meter readers and one part-time what fills in for vacations and cut-off days.
Mr. Daeke then asked about the increase in the Water Administration Fund. Manager Blackmon
said this increase was due to the salary increases being considered for certain positions.
Ms. Bennett said she recently had one meter reader leave but she was able to fill the position the
next day.
There was then a short discussion about the Engineering position and how this position is paid
partially out of the Water Fund because the position supports several other departments.
Council Member Williams asked if citizens can now make payments on-line. Ms. Bennett said
the real-time balances are now available.
Council Member Daeke requested information regarding what equipment has not been funded in
this proposed budget and said he thought this was important for Council to know what may need
to be purchased in future budgets.
There was no further discussion about the Water Fund, so the Mayor asked for a consensus for
the Water Fund. Other than the changes recommended above, it was the consensus of Council
to move forward with the recommended budget for the Water Fund of $7,530,571.
Sewer Fund
Finance Director Joey Fuqua said this Fund is more on task than the other two. He said there is
just a slight adjustment due to chemical costs, maintenance repairs and equipment. Mr. Fuqua
said this Fund included a SCADA upgrade and the replacement of one pump. This Fund shares
the cost of the HVAC system and the ice machine along with several other departments.
Council Member Coffey asked how the City continues to replace equipment without a rate
increase. Mr. Blackmon said the City has decent reserves and at some point there will be a need
to look at increasing rates; however, the current rates are meeting the current needs.

Minute Book 45
16 May 2022 Budget Work Session #1 Minutes

Mr. Blackmon added that there will be increases to the water rates once the expansion occurs at
the KLRW plant. He also mentioned the valuation of property that will come in 2024. He said
property values are increasing and he sees 2023/24 as critical years for increases.
There was a short discussion about I&I capital outlay costs, smoke testing and the need for
accountability by landlords/property owners to fix sewer problems on private property. Mr.
Perkinson said if the smoke test indicates there is a problem on private property, citizens are
given a certain number of days to fix the problem. Otherwise, a letter is provided the resident
that the City will fix the problem and charge the homeowner for the work.
There was no other discussion, so the Mayor asked for a consensus on the proposed budget of
$5,567,907. It was the consensus of Council to accept this budget figure.
CIP and Capital Reserve
Finance Director Fuqua distributed two handouts. One specifically showing the Capital Reserve
Utilities, Capital Reserve General and the Rate Stabilization Fund. The other handout, relating
to the ARPA funds received, showed a summary of suggested projects for using the funds. Mr.
Fuqua said the ARPA funds need to be designated by 2024 and spent by 2026.
Council Member Elliott asked about using a portion of these funds for housing. Manager.
Blackmon said $1.5M of ARPA funds have been set aside for redevelopment and staff will come
back throughout the year with projects for these funds. One project Mr. Blackmon mentioned is
setting aside a portion of these funds for property acquisition and urgent repairs. He said he
would like to buy back properties, if the County is unwilling to give them to the City, that are
currently city/county owned.
Council Member Rainey asked if the Recreation project costs would be split with the County
since Recreation and Parks functions under both the City and County. This led to a discussion
about how the County should be willing to turn over the city/county owned properties.
Other
Council Member Daeke asked how much was spent on paving last year. Only one small street
was paved. Council felt ARPA funds needed to be spent on paving along with Powell Bill funds.
Council Member Elliott felt streets in Ward 3 were the worst and ultimately it was decided that
Council Members should submit lists of streets they felt should be paved in FY 2023.
There was a short discussion about the Henderson Laundry. Manager Blackmon said there are
issues now revolving around the gentleman who purchased the laundry who has since passed
away. The laundry is in a trust and the parties do not seem to be on the same page for the
demolition of the structure. He said he is working with the City Attorney for options so the
parcel can ultimately be redeveloped without the city/county owning the property. Council

City Council Minutes
Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #2
19 May 2022
PRESENT
Mayor Eddie Ellington, Presiding; and Council Members Marion B. Williams, Sara M. Coffey,
William Burnette, Melissa Elliott and Garry D. Daeke
ABSENT
Council Members Rainey, Thorpe-Cooper and Spriggs
STAFF PRESENT
City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon, Finance Director Joey Fuqua, City Clerk Esther J.
McCrackin, Assistant City Manager Paylor Spruill, Police Chief Marcus Barrow, Fire Chief
Steve Cordell, Assistant Fire Chief Curtis Tyndall, Recreation and Parks Director Kendrick
Vann, Kerr Lake Regional and Henderson Water Reclamation Manager Christy Lipscomb,
Human Resources Director Elgin Lane, Development Services Director Corey Williams, Public
Works Director Andy Perkinson, Assistant Public Works Director Lee Owen, Water Collections
Manager Shay Bennett and Downtown Development Manager Tracy Madigan,
CALL TO ORDER
The 19 May 2022 Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #1 was called to order by
Mayor Eddie Ellington at 6:03 p.m. in the R. G. “Chick” Young, Jr. Council Chambers,
Municipal Building, 134 Rose Avenue, Henderson, NC.
ROLL CALL
The City Clerk called the roll and advised Mayor Ellington a quorum was present.
OPENING REMARKS
Mayor Ellington asked City Manager turned the meeting over to the City Manager.

Minute Book 45
19 May 2022 Budget Work Session #2 Minutes
Page 1 of 7

City Manager Blackmon asked that Council ask questions as the Finance Director goes through
the General Fund. He then asked Finance Director Joey Fuqua to begin the presentation.
Mr. Fuqua said the proposed budget looks very similar to the FY 22 budget. He said most of the
increases related to Premium Pay changes. The ARPA Funds have provided a good cushion for
both FY22 and FY 23

Review and Discussion of:
General Fund:
Mr. Fuqua said the General Fund is the largest task of putting together the budget. It has the
largest gaps between expenditures and revenues. He said staff starts the process by putting
together a matrix which is reviewed and adjusted. Then a revenue forecast is compiled and this
year the gap was over $3-4M. He said when they first started working on the proposed budget,
the final rulings for the use of ARPA funds had not been released. However, the final ruling
came in January, and it allows a lot of latitude on how the ARPA funds can be used. Mr. Fuqua
then moved through the various departments making up the General Fund.
Mr. Fuqua said the first notable change is with the IT Department. There was a notable increase
as the Manager position is vacant and depending on NCOL and contracting needs, a significant
increase was included to ensure the IT needs are taken care of until a manager is brought on
board.
The Police Department will be replacing vehicles and the budget includes the cost of upfitting
those vehicles.
The next significant budget change comes in the Fire Department which includes a new fire
truck. Mr. Blackmon stated that this equipment is needed as they have been constantly patching
and repairing existing equipment.
No major equipment is included in the proposed budget for the Public Works Department.
However, the budget includes a new roof at the Operations Center, along with a new ice
machine. The cost of the ice machine will be shared with several other departments.
Mr. Fuqua said the Garage costs increased mainly due to fuel costs. This account includes all the
departments with vehicles and is closely monitored as fuel costs are very volatile at this time.
Council Member Coffey asked if staff felt the increase is sufficient. Mr. Fuqua said they have
included a cushion of $4.30 per gallon and although the City receives fuel at a discounted rate it
may have to use all the budgeted amount with the way fuel prices are increasing due to the war
in Ukraine.

Minute Book 45
19 May 2022 Budget Work Session #2 Minutes
Page 2 of 7

The Street Fund includes an increase due to hardware/software licenses/permits.
The Sanitation Fund includes the annual increase from GFL (formerly Waste Industries) and also
the costs of licenses and permits.
The expanded programs in the Recreation and Parks Department require additional supplies to
fund these new initiatives. Also included in this departmental budget is building maintenance
along with hardware/software needs
Council Member Coffey asked if the budget includes repairs to the tennis courts and the pool
area. Mr. Blackmon said the tennis courts have been repaired, along with the parking lot. He
said if there are safety issues in any area, they are addressed as soon as possible.
Council Member Elliott asked if the County is contributing their portion to the Recreation and
Parks Department. Mr. Blackmon said yes and explained that once the City establishes its
budget for this department it is sent to the County so they can budget their portion.
Council Member Daeke shared that the Recreation and Parks Department budget comes from
55% of the City and 45% from the County.
Mention of City/County shared projects took the discussion to other areas with Mr. Daeke asking
what the County is responsible for and if it is paying its share. Mr. Blackmon said the County
pays for Youth Services. There was a brief discussion about the Library and Mr. Daeke
suggested a meeting with the County to review shared projects. Mr. Blackmon said that
McGregor Hall has requested a new HVAC system. He added that with the Recreation and
Parks Department, the City bills the County for their portion.
Mr. Fuqua then turned the discussion to Local Agency requests. He said no change has been
made in these requests.
Council Member Burnette asked if the City makes any contribution to McGregor Hall. Mr.
Blackmon said McGregor Hall did not make an official request for funding but added that there
are some funds held in a Rec Players account that he advised Mr. Mark Hopper to make a formal
for as the account holds approximately $6,000.
Council Member Daeke suggested a discussion with McGregor Hall’s staff once the pavilion is
up and running. Mr. Blackmon said he recently met with the McGregor Hall staff and this was
part of their discussion. The Manager said he will be presenting the final design for the pavilion
soon and sees some events as better suited for outdoors rather than inside McGregor Hall, such
as Sunday concerts in the park.

Minute Book 45
19 May 2022 Budget Work Session #2 Minutes
Page 3 of 7

There was a brief discussion about redevelopment dollars being used for services such as
homelessness. The Manager said the City must be careful because it does not provide human
services per se, but it has set aside funds that will provide services that will need to be
implemented by community-based organizations. Ms. Elliott asked for the expenditure deadline
of the ARPA funds and was told the deadline is 2026. Mr. Blackmon said Council will need to
approve the use of the funds and a process needs to be set in place.
Council Member Williams asked for an explanation of several large increases in the General
Fund. Her first question led to a review of the IT departmental increase. The Manager said the
City has a two-year contract with NCOL for IT assistance 24 hours a week. The City will recruit
a new director that will work closely with NCOL once hired.
Council Member Williams then asked about the increase in Downtown Development. Mr.
Blackmon said the majority of the increase in this department, as with most of the funds, is from
the 2% salary increase and then the 3% Premium Pay.
Council Member Williams then asked about the Police and Fire increases. Mr. Blackmon
explained these are the two largest departments and the majority of the costs relate to salaries.
He added the last year Council approved six new positions for the Fire Department. Also, the
purchase of a new fire truck. Additional costs for both departments include uniforms, repairs,
travel, dues, etc.
City Manager Blackmon said he appreciates each department. He said the Fire Department’s
goal is to identify a location for the needed new fire station and to hire another 6 staff members.
He said locations are being considered but these goals are not included in the FY23 budget.
The Manager envisions having to increase taxes in the coming years as the ARPA funds dwindle,
salaries increase, and work begins on the KLRW renovations. He said tough decisions have to
be made.
Finance Director Joey Fuqua said the ARPA funds have helped to cover the current expenses but
going forward the City will need to absorb many costs and the City needs to find savings
wherever it can.
Council Member Coffey said there is still a need for more firemen and Mr. Fuqua said some of
the priorities are being taken care of now so those additional staff members can be hired in the
coming years.
Fire Chief Cordell said the ladder truck has been out of service 88 days at a cost of $61,000 for
repairs.

A general discussion began with Council Member Daeke asking what a one cent tax raise would

Minute Book 45
19 May 2022 Budget Work Session #2 Minutes
Page 4 of 7

garner. Mr. Fuqua told him approximately $88,000.
Mr. Fuqua said the capital outlay for a new fire truck is approximately $1.3M.
Mr. Fuqua said the LGC is in the process of reviewing the proposed budget and seems satisfied
with what Council is considering for approval. He stated the second installment of the ARPA
funds should be received shortly. However, if the funds are not received before the budget is
approved in June, he will have to review those funds from the line item, but they will be added
back once received.
Plans to place a columbarium in Elmwood Cemetery have not been finalized. Mr. Blackmon
said the City Attorney has verified the funds in the Trust Fund can be used for repair and
improvements in the cemetery including a columbarium.
Police Chief Barrow said the Police Department now functions in the world of technology so
many of the departmental costs have increased due to upgrading software and the need to
analyze data more efficiently. The Chief said Body Cams need to be upgraded yearly. Council
Member Daeke commented that grants are wonderful, but they do cost money “down the road.”
Mr. Daeke asked for a list of equipment that was not funded in the proposed budget as both Ms.

what the Manager included in the budget. Ms. Coffey felt equipment should be ordered now

said yes and went on to say $1M has been set for this purpose.
In summary, Mr. Blackmon said there has been good discussion and asked if there were any

The issue of Asset Forfeiture was brought up and the Chief said although there are currently no
funds in the account, the new taskforce has made some stops that should bring more than
$100,000 to the City soon.
Council Member Elliott asked why the Airport received so much funding. It was explained that
the airport is owned by Vance County, Henderson, Granville County and Oxford and that the
amount included in the budget is the City’s portion of running the airport.
Ms. Elliott also
asked about the Corbitt Museum. Mr. Daeke said the City purchased a home owned by Mr.
Bennett Perry when the County said it was not interested so what is included in the budget
covers insurance and other costs.

Minute Book 45
19 May 2022 Budget Work Session #2 Minutes
Page 5 of 7

Car Park Items:
Salary Adjustments – Mr. Blackmon said his recommendation for several departments that have
employees that are under paid is to increase their hourly rate by $1.00. There was very little
discussion as Council seemed to agree that the $1.00 increase provides a better wage for
employees than offering a percent increase.
Retirement Expenses – Mr. Blackmon said there are 35 employees who can retire within the next
year or two at the age of 50 or 55. The issue is whether the City should provide full insurance
coverage for these employees until they reach age 65. Mr. Blackmon said if the City carried all
35 employees for 10 years, at the current health insurance rate of just under $700.00 per
employee, the City’s cost would be over $3M. Another option is to allow retirees to use their
sick time for health coverage rather than State retirement. Mr. Blackmon said this is more
difficult to calculate but mentioned it was not as equitable as some employees could have 1,000
hours of sick time where others might only have 300 hours.
There was a lengthy discussion about whether changing the existing policy would be for the best
and how the policy should be changed. Council Member Daeke suggested Council hold a work
session to further discuss this matter and asked the Manager to contact other municipalities to see
how they are handling retirement, determine costs and what to do about individuals who have
already retired.
(Clerk’s Note: Council Member Daeke left the meeting at 7:31 p.m.
City Manager Blackmon said he would investigate what other communities are doing and
reminded Council that retirees have options to continue coverage with the City…just not at
100%.
At this time, Mayor Ellington asked if Council was comfortable with the budget as presented.
The consensus was they were satisfied with the budget as presented.
Mr. Blackmon reminded Council that Monday, May 23, 2022, at 6:00 will be the next Work
Session and it will also be the Public Hearing.

City Council Minutes
Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #3
23 May 2022
PRESENT
Mayor Eddie Ellington, Presiding; and Council Members Sara M. Coffey, William Burnette,
Garry D. Daeke, Ola Thorpe-Cooper, and Jason A. Spriggs
ABSENT
Council Member Williams, Elliott and Rainey
STAFF PRESENT
City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon, Finance Director Joey Fuqua, City Clerk Esther J.
McCrackin, Assistant City Manager Paylor Spruill, Kerr Lake Regional Water and Henderson
Water Reclamation Director Christy Lipscomb, Public Works Director Andy Perkinson and
Assistant Public Works Director Lee Owen, Fire Chief Steve Cordell, Assistant Fire Chief Curtis
Tyndall, Police Chief Marcus Barrow, Recreation and Parks Director Kendrick Vann,
Development Services Director Corey Williams and Downtown Development Manager Tracy
Madigan
CALL TO ORDER
The 23 May 2022 Henderson City Council Budget Work Session #1 was called to order by
Mayor Eddie Ellington at 6:23 p.m. in the R. G. “Chick” Young, Jr. Council Chambers,
Municipal Building, 134 Rose Avenue, Henderson, NC.
ROLL CALL
The City Clerk called the roll and advised Mayor Ellington a quorum was present.
OPENING REMARKS
Mayor Ellington apologized for the delay and thanked everyone for being patient. He then
referred the meeting over to the Manager.

Minute Book 45
23 May 2022 Budget Work Session #3 Minutes
Page 1 of 4

City Manager Blackmon said by law, the Public Hearing must be held at least ten (10) days prior
to adopting the budget. He reminded Council that they had the first Work Session on May 16
and then another on May 19. During those meetings the Enterprise Funds, General Fund, Powell
Bill and CIP/Capital Reserve Funds have all been reviewed. The proposed FY 22-23 Budget
contains no property tax increase, and no Water, Sewer or Regional Water increases. Mr.
Blackmon proposed that the Mayor conduct the Public Hearing and then any outstanding items
can be discussed.
(Clerk’s Note: This Public Hearing was properly advertised, and citizens were asked to submit
questions/comments by 3:00 p.m. the day of the meeting or to attend the meeting in person.)
Conduct Budget Public Hearing on FY 20-21 Budget:
Mayor Ellington opened the public hearing by asking if anyone wished to speak in favor of the
Proposed FY 22-23 budget. No one wished to address Council in favor of the budget, so he
asked if anyone wished to speak against the Proposed FY22-23 budget. Again, no one came
forward.
(Clerk’s Note: This meeting was held via Zoom, and no one logged on to the Zoom platform
during this meeting.)
Mayor Ellington closed the Public Hearing and again turned the meeting over to the Manager.
OTHER
Council Member Coffey asked that a part-time animal control officer be added to the budget.
She feels there are more animals running loose within the City than in the County. She thought
the three Vance County officers were insufficient to cover the entire County.
Police Chief Marcus Barrow stated that normally Vance County Animal Control officers respond
to calls but if there is a criminal offense involved within the City, the Police are called. He said
he did not have the details with him but that he had mentioned this during the Retreat and would
be happy to provide his information.
After a brief discussion about how other communities handle controlling animals, who the parttime officer would report to and why the County has not been asked to increase their staffing,
Council Member Daeke recommended this concern be presented to the Public Safety Committee
for further consideration.

Minute Book 45
23 May 2022 Budget Work Session #3 Minutes
Page 2 of 4

Council Member Daeke felt the Committee of the Whole needed to be cognizant of citizen
dollars and mentioned the need for additional staffing in the Code Compliance (Development
Services) department.
Mayor Ellington concluded the discussion by saying the Manager will gather information to
present to the Public Safety Committee and then this will come back to Council.
Manager Blackmon said if an officer is approved, details will need to be worked out with the
County, as for one thing, the City has no facility to hold animals.
Council Member Coffey thanked everyone for listening to her concern and is looking forward to
the Public Safety meeting.
City Manager Blackmon then turned their attention to the retirement issue that was “Car Parked”
during earlier budget discussions. He said he would like to hold a Special Called Meeting to
have a Closed Work Session once he and staff have time to gather and review more information
regarding the retirement concerns. He asked the Clerk to distribute the Memo from Police Chief
Barrow regarding using sick time for insurance, along with minutes and resolutions from the
early 2000’s when Council made changes to the existing policy. Mr. Blackmon felt this would
avoid making rash decisions and that the decisions made would be well documented.
Council Member Burnette asked for verification that the Public Works would be receiving a
salary increase to help retain employees. Mr. Blackmon said not only would employees in the
Public Works department receive a $1.00 increase, but this would also affect the wastewater and
water employees.
Mr. Blackmon said he foresees a salary study coming within the next few years and adjustments
may be needed at that time.
Mayor Ellington asked Council if they were satisfied with the budget as proposed, including the
salary adjustment. The Consensus of those present was that there was no need for future Work
Sessions and that they felt this was the budget that should be presented for approval at the June
13, 2022, meeting.
The Manager reminded Council that until the budget is approved, he is open for discussion on
any item.
Mayor Ellington thanked the Manager, Finance Director and staff for all they do and what was
done to put together the FY 22-23 budget.

Minute Book 45
23 May 2022 Budget Work Session #3 Minutes
Page 3 of 4

ADJOURNMENT
Council Member Coffey moved for adjournment. Motion seconded by both Council Member
Spriggs and Daeke, and unanimously approved. The meeting adjourned at 6:53 p.m.

City Council Minutes
Regular Meeting
June 13, 2022
PRESENT
Mayor Eddie Ellington, Presiding; and Council Members Marion B Williams, Sara M. Coffey,
William Burnette, D. Michael Rainey, Melissa Elliott, Ola Thorpe-Cooper and Jason A. Spriggs.
ABSENT: Council Member Garry Daeke
STAFF PRESENT
City Manager Edward T. Blackmon, City Attorney D. Rix Edwards, City Clerk Esther J.
McCrackin, Finance Director Joey Fuqua, Fire Chief Steve Cordell, Assistant Fire Chief Curtis
Tyndall, Development Services Director Corey Williams, Human Resources Director Elgin
Lane, Public Works Director Andy Perkinson, Kerr Lake Regional Water and Henderson Water
Reclamation Director Christy Lipscomb, Customer Service Manager Shay Bennett and
Downtown Development Manager Tracy Madigan
CALL TO ORDER
The 11 June 2022 Regular Meeting of the Henderson City Council was called to order by Mayor
Eddie Ellington at 6:00 p.m. in the R. G. “Chick” Young, Jr. Council Chambers, Municipal
Building, 134 Rose Avenue, Henderson, NC.
ROLL CALL
The City Clerk called the roll and stated that a quorum was present.
INVOCATION AND PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE
Council Member Burnette led the prayer and the Pledge of Allegiance.
OPENING COMMENTS
Mayor Ellington extended a warm welcome to everyone in attendance.
ADJUSTMENTS TO/APPROVAL OF AGENDA
Mayor Ellington asked if there were any adjustments to the Agenda. City Clerk McCrackin said
there would be an add-on item to the Consent Agenda (Accept Bid Proposal from Insight
Planning and Development to Administer Community Development Block Grant CDBG #20C3633). Council Member Burnette moved to accept the Agenda as adjusted. Motion seconded
by Council Member Elliott and unanimously approved.

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes
Page 1 of 12

APPROVAL OF MINUTES
Mayor Ellington asked for any corrections to and/or approval of the minutes. Council Member
Burnette moved the approval of the 9 May Regular Meeting minutes, the 16 May Budget Work
Session #1 minutes, 19 May Budget Work Session #2 minutes and the 23 May Budget Work
Session #3 minutes. Motion seconded by Council Member Coffey and unanimously approved.
PRESENTATIONS/RECOGNITIONS
Fire Chief Steve Cordell and Assistant Fire Chief Curtis Tyndall presented medals of
commendation to five firefighters this evening. Captain Joshua A Bolton was presented a Medal
of Valor for his actions to preserve the life of another during conditions that were extremely lifethreatening for himself as well as the victim trapped inside the structure. Capt. Bolton sustained
second degree burns to areas of his shoulders and arm with some portions being considered third
degree burns. The Life Saving Medal which is awarded to members of the department who save
the life of another person through proper actions was presented this evening to Captain Kyle
Holtzman, Master Firefighter Watkins Renn Hale, Senior Firefighter Zack Short, and Firefighter
William Breedlove for their part in locating and removing the victim from the burning house. A
standing ovation was given for each of these individuals. Chief Cordell ‘s entire presentation is
incorporated by reference and hereby made part of these minutes.
Council Member Coffey thanked the department for all they do, and Council Member Burnette
mentioned that while citizens run from a fire, these individuals run into a fire. Mayor Ellington
thanked the Chief for his moving presentation and stated how much we all appreciate what this
department does daily.
PUBLIC HEARING
Adopt the Comprehensive Plan for the City of Henderson (Reference: CAF 22-62;
Ordinance 22-12)
The City Manager reminded Council that this project started a year ago and tonight is the
culmination of the work done. He then asked Development Services Director Corey Williams to
introduce the Insight Planning and Development staff member who will make a brief
presentation of the plan updates.
Mr. Williams then introduced Mr. Chip Bartlett who explained that Phase I of this project was
the kickoff which took place between June and September 2021. Phase II was the plan
development and public engagement portion of the project that took place between September
2021 and March 2022. Tonight, Phase III is the completion, review and adoption portion of the
project that began in March 2022. Mr. Bartlett said the citizens of Henderson had a lot to say
during the public engagement portion as there were over 5,000 responses (every time someone
clicked) to the citizens survey. He said there was a good cross section of participants and the top
three assets mentioned were Kerr Lake, proximity to Raleigh, Wake Forest and Vance-Granville
Community College.
The top three issues facing the city’s future were redevelopment of
distressed neighborhoods, economic opportunities-jobs and public safety. To keep the plan
transparent, Mr. Bartlett mentioned edits that have been made since the Planning Board approved

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes
Page 2 of 12

the plan in its entirety and he said he would be happy to answer any questions Council may have
at this time.
Council Member Elliott asked how many individuals responded to the survey. Mr. Bartlett said
most questions had 150-200 responses.
With no further questions, Mayor Ellington opened the Public Hearing by asking if anyone was
present who wished to speak in favor of this ordinance. No one else came forward, so Mayor
Ellington then asked if anyone was present who wished to speak in opposition to the ordinance.
No one came forward. Mayor Ellington closed the Public Hearing and asked for Council’s
pleasure.
Council Member Elliott moved the approval of Ordinance 22-12, Adopt the Comprehensive Plan
for the City of Henderson. Motion seconded by Council Member Rainey and APPROVED by
the following vote: YES: Williams, Coffey, Burnette, Rainey, Elliott, Thorpe-Cooper and
Spriggs. NO: None. ABSTAIN: None. ABSENT: Daeke. (See Ordinance Book 10, p 923)
Amending the City Code and Zoning Ordinance to Allow the Planning Board and Board of
Adjustment Extraterritorial Jurisdiction (ETJ) Members Full Voting Privileges on City
and ETJ Matters (Reference: CAF 22-54; Ordinance 22-08, Ordinance 22-09)
City Manager Blackmon said this matter came before the Boards and Commissions Committee
and the recommendation was to allow ETJ members voting privileges. He said the ETJ members
are valuable members of the Planning Board and Board of Adjustment and it was strongly felt
these members should be allowed to vote.
With no discussion, Mayor Ellington opened the Public Hearing by asking if anyone was present
who wished to speak in favor of this rezoning. No one else came forward to speak in favor of
this rezoning so Mayor Ellington then asked if anyone was present who wished to speak in
opposition to the rezoning. No one came forward.
Mayor Ellington then closed the Public Hearing and asked for Council’s pleasure.
Council Member Coffey moved the approval of Ordinance 22-08 and Ordinance 22-09
Amending the City Code and Zoning Ordinance to Allow the Planning Board and Board of
Adjustment Extraterritorial Jurisdiction (ETJ) Members Full Voting Privileges on City and ETJ
Matters. Motion seconded by Council Member Burnette and APPROVED by the following
vote: YES: Coffey, Burnette, Rainey, Elliott, Thorpe-Cooper, Spriggs and Williams. NO: None.
ABSTAIN: None. ABSENT: Daeke. (See Ordinance Book 10, p 915 and 917)
PUBLIC COMMENT PERIOD ON AGENDA ITEMS
City Clerk McCrackin advised no citizen wished to address Council on Agenda items.

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes

NEW BUSINESS
Adoption of the FY 22-23 Budget and Annual Fee Schedule (Reference: CAF 22-55;
Ordinance 22-10)
The City Clerk reminded Council that during the budget work sessions, Finance Director Joey
Fuqua stated that if the ARPA funds were not received before the budget was adopted, an
adjustment would need to be made to the General Fund. As of today, the ARPA funds have not
been received so the Clerk distributed a corrected budget page and said when the funds are
received, the Finance Director will adjust the budget accordingly.
While the Clerk was distributing the updated information, Mayor Ellington thanked the Manager,
Finance Director and staff for their efforts in compiling the FY22-23 budget.
City Manager Blackmon also thanked the Finance Director and staff for helping put together the
budget. He reminded Council that the proposed budget was presented on May 9 with three Work
Sessions. The Public Hearing was held on May 23 and as required by law; the budget can be
adopted as it has been more than 10 days since that Hearing.
The FY22-23 budget contains no increase in taxes, and no material changes to the Fee Schedule.
BCBS was selected for health care to keep costs down and he said the City will still offer health
care reimbursement/subsidy for spouse and family coverage. Of significance is the $1.5M
Council approved for redevelopment. He said this indicates Council’s foresight. Also, the
budget includes increases for front line workers. In conclusion, he said staff understands they
cannot always get what they want and he felt the budget was the best it could be with the funds
available.
Mayor Ellington asked Council how it would like to proceed.
Council Member Rainey moved the approval of Ordinance 22-10, Adoption of the FY 22-23
Budget and Annual Fee Schedule. Motion seconded by Council Member Burnette and
APPROVED by the following vote: YES: Burnette, Rainey, Elliott, Thorpe-Cooper, Spriggs,
Williams and Coffey. NO: None. ABSTAIN: None. ABSENT: Daeke. (See Ordinance Book
10, p 919)
Acceptance of 20 MGD Guaranteed Maximum Price (GMP) with CDM Smith (Reference
CAF 22-63; Resolution 22-51)
City Manager Blackmon said there seems to be a light at the end of the proverbial tunnel
regarding the KLRW expansion. The KLRW Advisory Board voted to accept the GMP for the
upgrade and funding has been secured for the project. The next step is to obtain the Local
Government Commission approval that he hopes will happen within the next few months.
Permits will then need to be obtained and the actual expansion should begin early in 2023. At
this time no rate changes are scheduled but there will be increases once the $79,605,000 project
begins.
There were no questions so Mayor Ellington asked Council how it would like to proceed.

Funding Transfer for Tar-Pamlico Stormwater Ordinance Revisions (Reference: CAF 2260; Resolution 22-49, Ordinance 22-11)
City Manager Blackmon explained the stormwater plan needs to be revised to stay in
compliance. Since the City does not have a stormwater utility fund, dollars need to be
transferred to update the plan. Mr. Blackmon said once the State approves the plan, the City will
have five years to meet all the State requirements which will involve additional staffing among
other things that will appear in the FY23-24 budget.
There were no questions, so Mayor Ellington asked Council how it wished to proceed.
Council Member Elliott moved the approval of Resolution 22-49, Funding Transfer for TarPamlico Stormwater Ordinance Revisions and Ordinance 22-11, Establishing Funds for TarPamlico Stormwater Ordinance Revisions. Motion seconded by Council Member Coffey and
APPROVED by the following vote: YES: Thorpe-Cooper, Spriggs, Williams, Coffey, Burnette,
Rainey and Elliott. NO: None. ABSTAIN: None. ABSENT: Daeke. (See Resolution Book 7, p
285; Ordinance Book 10, p 921)

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes
Page 5 of 12

Seventh Amendment to the Franklin County Water Agreement (Reference: CAF 22-51.1;
Resolution 22-43)
City Manager Blackmon reminded Council that during the April 12, 2021, meeting, a Sixth
Amendment to this agreement was approved that increased the supply of water by 450,000 GPD
to 3.45MGD. The price remained at $4.37 per gallon and the term of that agreement was for 36
months from the date of execution or to the date the KLRW plan upgrade was completed. Due
to the delay Franklin County has requested an additional 48 months beyond the expiration date
of the Sixth Amendment. Both the Mayor and Manager felt an extension was an act of good
will. Mr. Blackmon said it makes sense to approve this amendment to allow Franklin County to
continue to receive water that will assist with their development while the City will continue to
receive additional income.
There were no questions so Mayor Ellington now asked Council how it would like to proceed.
Council Member Spriggs moved the approval of Resolution 22-43.1, Seventh Amendment to the
Franklin County Water Agreement.
Motion seconded by Council Member Rainey and
APPROVED by the following vote: YES: Thorpe-Cooper, Spriggs, Williams, Coffey, Burnette,
Rainey and Elliott. NO: None. ABSTAIN: None. ABSENT: Daeke. (See Resolution Book 7, p
273)

meeting)

individual has a PhD in nutritional biochemistry, and she prefers working in
rural areas as they do not have access to healthy, nutritious food.
A resolution was passed unanimously by the State to name a portion of I-85
for Dr. Andrea Harris. Her foundation submitted the request which required
a resolution from a government entity which the City of Henderson was
happy to provide.

c) City Attorney – No Report
d) City Clerk – No Report

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes
Page 10 of 12

Good evening, Mayor, City Council Members, City Manager, and staff. Thank you for allowing
the Fire Department to share with this assembly an account of a significant event that occurred
this past January.
On the evening of January 16th, 2022, at 8:00 pm, fire stations 1 and 2 were dispatched to a
residence for a reported structure fire with smoke and flames visible with two occupants in the
house. Engine 1, the Battalion Chief (Battalion 1), and Ladder 1 responded from station 1.
Engine 4 responded from station 2 and all units responded emergency traffic to the scene. While
the fire units were en route, the Vance County Communications Center advised that there was a
Henderson Police Officer on scene who stated that there was fire coming from the back of the
house and that there was an occupant still inside the dwelling that could not get out. The
Battalion Chief and Engine 1, arrived on scene at 8:02, followed shortly by Ladder 1 at 8:03, and
Engine 4 at 8:04. The Battalion Chief established command and marked the fire as a working
house fire and asked for central to initiate a priority one callback which is a procedure where off
duty personnel are called back in to work to staff an engine that can be ready to answer any
further emergency calls.
Engine 1 personnel deployed a 1 3/4" hand line toward the rear of the right side of the structure
while Battalion Chief Twisdale completed a 360 degree walk around. A second line was
deployed for the rapid intervention team. Heavy fire and smoke conditions were found all along
the rear of the 1125 square foot, single family dwelling, with lighter smoke presenting from the
eaves on all other sides of the structure. Ladder company personnel attempted to open the front
as well as the left side door to gain access to the structure and were met with hoarder conditions
with belongings stacked up completely blocking both doorways from floor to ceiling. Captain
Bolton, the officer in charge on Engine 1, made his way to the back door under the covered
carport area while Firefighter Ramos stretched the hose line and positioned it at the back door.
Captain Bolton was informed that the resident who had gotten out was saying that her child was
still inside. Upon hearing this, Captain Bolton made a split-second decision to do a quick sweep
of the kitchen and dining area, looking for the victim that was reportedly trapped inside near the
area involved in fire. He was met with fire conditions that were unbearable but did clear two
rooms before the heat and flames became too intense to bear, sustaining heavy damage to his
personal protective gear and burns to his shoulders and upper arm area. He immediately exited
the structure and notified command of his injury. After being injured, Captain Bolton considered
re-entering the structure but was escorted to an ambulance for assessment. EMS began treating
him and he was transported to the UNC burn center for assessment and treatment. Immediately
after his exit, firefighters knowing that the fire would continue to increase in volume and
intensity knew they had to begin suppressing the fire so that they could re-enter the dwelling to
complete a primary search. When the firefighters resumed the search, they found a female victim
just inside the living room adjacent to fire area. The victim was found face down on the floor in
front of a recliner surrounded by clutter that made it difficult to locate her. The 61year old female
was removed by firefighters through the back door. After being removed from the house, patient
care was transferred to Vance Co EMS.
Captain Joshua A Bolton attempted to rescue a trapped victim inside of a burning structure
despite the threat of personal injury and attempted to find the trapped victim inside the home. He
did this in conditions that were so extreme that he sustained second degree burns to areas of his
shoulders and arm with some portions being third degree. During this act of heroism, he did not

Minute Book 45
13 June 2022 Regular Virtual Meeting Minutes
Page 11 of 12

reach the victim, but helped to rule out the most intense fire areas and allowed the remaining
personnel to search the adjacent areas where the victim was found. After a lengthy hospital stay,
the victim is out and receiving rehabilitation care in a local facility. She has spoken with Captain
Bolton by telephone since her discharge from the hospital. Captain Bolton’s split-second
decision to search the rear of the structure without regard to his own personal safety allowed his
crew to continue the search and locate the victim where he left off. His decision to enter the
structure before the application of water was made for the purpose of protecting the victim from
deadly steam that would have been generated by the water. If the victim would have been in the
back rooms, the outcome would have been worse for the victim if the application of water had
been made prior to her removal. It is the opinion of the commendation committee and the Fire
Chief that Captain Joshua A Bolton be granted a medal of valor for his actions to preserve or
attempt to preserve the life of another during conditions that were extremely life-threatening for
himself as well as the victim trapped inside the structure. It is also recognized that not only did
Captain Bolton risk his life for the trapped victim, but he endured the stresses of intense fire and
heat and kept other members of his crew just outside the door so they would not be exposed to
the extreme conditions.
The committee also recognizes that the successful rescue of the fire victim was accomplished by
the efforts of both engine companies along with the ladder company under the leadership of
Battalion Chief Tim Twisdale. This team’s efforts were successful due to many factors. One
factor was that the victim had the benefit of competent and brave professionals coming for her
that continually train for every conceivable contingency in such an event as this one. Another
major factor was that the victim was fortunate enough to live just ½ mile from the closest fire
station which is certainly not the case for all properties in this city. Given the actions of the
following personnel it is the opinion of the committee and the Fire Chief that the following
commendations are to be made: The Life Saving Medal which is awarded to members of our
department who saves the life of another person through proper actions is to be presented to
Captain Kyle Holtzman, Master Firefighter Watkins Renn Hale, Senior Firefighter Zack Short,
and Firefighter William Breedlove for their part in locating and removing the victim from the
burning house. While not receiving a commendation medal today, the following personnel
should also be recognized. Captain William Boyd, Engineer Dylan Dickerson, Firefighter
William Ramos, and Firefighter Devin Sherrod who performed fire suppression, forcible entry
and search functions while Master Firefighter Trenton Clark operated the pump on Engine 1 that
provided the vital fire streams at this incident. With the council’s permission we would like to
present the gentlemen receiving their commendation metals to you now.
By Fire Chief Steve Cordell

CAF 22-62

Adopt the Comprehensive Plan for the City of Henderson

Requested by: Development Services Director Corey Williams
Explanation: Chapter 160D, Article 5 of the North Carolina General Statues requires adoption
and reasonable maintenance of a comprehensive plan as a condition of adopting
and applying zoning regulations. The Comprehensive Plan is intended to set forth
goals, policies, and programs to guide the present and future physical, social, and
economic development of the jurisdiction. A comprehensive or land use plan
uses text and maps to designate the future use or reuse of land. A land use plan is
intended to guide coordinated, efficient, and orderly development within the
planning and development regulation jurisdiction based on an analysis of present
and future needs.
Insight Planning and Development, the Planning Board, and staff have been
working on creating a new Comprehensive Plan since June 2021. There have
been several meetings throughout the year and City webpages dedicated to the
comprehensive plan development. A community public engagement meeting was
held March 22, 2022, at Aycock Recreation Center where over 70 citizens
attended and participated.
A copy of the Comprehensive Plan may be obtained through the Development
Services department.
Fiscal Note:

Comprehensive Plan development has cost $75,000, which was a part of the FY
22 budget.

13 June 2022 Agenda
Page 2 of 12

Recommendation: The Planning Board met on May 16, 2022, and unanimously approved the
recommendation to adopt the proposed Comprehensive Plan developed by Insight
Planning and Development.
Attachments: Ordinance 22-12
Planning Board Minutes

CAF 22-54

Amending the City Code and Zoning Ordinance to Allow the Planning Board
and Board of Adjustment Extraterritorial Jurisdiction (ETJ) Members Full
Voting Privileges on City and ETJ Matters

Requested by: Development Services Director Corey Williams
Explanation: The Planning Board and Board of Adjustment have had issues with establishing a
quorum for meetings. After discussion among board members and staff, the idea
of allowing ETJ members to vote on City related matters was brought before the
Boards and Commissions Committee in April and the Planning Board in May,
and both recommended allowing ETJ members full voting privileges on City and
ETJ matters.
Fiscal Note:

There is no financial impact. The City currently pays both City and ETJ board
members the same rate of pay.

Recommendation: Approved the Amendments to the Ordinances
Attachments: Ordinance 22-09
Ordinance 22-09
Planning Board Minutes 05-16-22

PUBLIC COMMENT PERIOD ON AGENDA ITEMS
Citizens may only speak on Agenda items at this time. Citizens wishing to address the Council
must sign-in on a form provided by the City Clerk prior to the beginning of the meeting. The
sign-in form is located on the podium. When recognized by the Mayor, come forward to the
podium, state your name, address and if you are a city resident. Please review the Citizen
Comment Guidelines that are provided below.3
3

Citizen Comment Guidelines for Agenda Items
The Mayor and City Council welcome and encourage citizens to attend City Council meetings and to offer comments on matters of concern to
them. Citizens are requested to review the following public comment guidelines prior to addressing the City Council.
1) Citizens are requested to limit their comments to three minutes; however, the Mayor, at his discretion, may limit comments to three minutes
should there appear to be a large number of people wishing to address the Council;
2) Comments should be presented in a civil manner and be non-personal in nature, fact-based and issue oriented. Except for the public hearing
comment period, citizens must speak for themselves during the public comment periods;
3) Citizens may not yield their time to another person;
4) Topics requiring further investigation will be referred to the appropriate city official, Council Committee or agency and may, if in order, be
scheduled for a future meeting agenda;
5) Individual personnel issues are confidential by law and will not be discussed. Complaints relative to specific individuals are to be directed to
the City Manager;
6) Comments involving matters related to an on-going police investigative matter and/or the court system will not be permitted; and
7) Citizens should not expect specific Council action, deliberation and/or comment on subject matter brought up during the public comment
section unless and until it has been scheduled as a business item on a future meeting agenda.

CAF 22—51.1 Approving the Seventh Amendment to the Franklin County Water
Agreement
Requested by: City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon
Explanation:

At its April 12, 2021, regular meeting, the Henderson City Council approved a
Sixth Amendment to the Franklin County Water Agreement that increased the
supply of water by 450,000 GPD to 3.45 MGD. The price remained at $4.37 per
gallon and the term of the agreement was for thirty-six (36) months from the date
of execution of the amendment by both parties; or to the date the Kerr Lake
Regional Water System Plant Upgrade is completed, this completion to be
determined by the City.
Due to the delay with the start of the project and the need for a consistent water
source for allocation purposes, Franklin County has requested that the Sixth
Amendment be modified to lengthen the term an additional forty-eight (48)
months beyond the expiration date of the executed Sixth Amendment. This
would be the Seventh Amendment to the original contract.

Fiscal Note:

The increase in water supply to Franklin County will generate additional revenue
for the City of Henderson.

Recommendation: Staff recommends that Council approve the Seventh Amendment of the
Franklin County Water Agreement by extending the term of the Sixth
Amendment forty-eight (48) months from the date of expiration of the Sixth
Amendment by the parties.
Attachments: Resolution 22-43.1
Seventh Amendment to Franklin County Water Agreement

1326, Page 0115, Further Identified as PIN #0058 03007, and 248 Denver
Street, Recorded in Vance County Tax Deed Book 1235 Page 9527, Further
Identified as PIN #0058 04002.

Requested by: City Manager E. Terrell Blackmon
Explanation: Vance County Board of Commissioners received an offer to purchase the
properties located at 0 Hall Street and 248 Denver Street. The sale of the property
will be taken before the Vance County Board during their June 2022 meeting.
The offer to purchase 0 Hall Street is $1,000. The assessed tax value is $6,000.
Unpaid County taxes total $480 and the unpaid City taxes total $370 for a total of
$850 in unpaid taxes. There is also $1,315 in accumulated interest and $3,000 in
Attorney fees associated with this property.
The offer to purchase 248 Denver Street is $1,000. The assessed tax value is
$6,800. Unpaid County taxes total $1,974.72 and the unpaid City taxes total
$1,120.06 for a total of $3,094.78 in unpaid taxes. There is also $525.22 in
accumulated interest and $2,955 in Attorney fees associated with this property.
Fiscal Note:

If Council approves the transfer of this property to Vance County, it will be with
the understanding upon the sale that the proceeds will be shared on a pro rata
basis.

CAF 22-57

Submission of an Application to the Bureau of Justice Assistance Bulletproof
Vest Program FY22 Grant

Requested by: Police Chief Marcus Barrow
Explanation: The Henderson Police Department (HPD) is seeking funding from the US
Department of Justice Bureau of Justice Assistance (BJA) Bulletproof Vest
Program (BVP) to reimburse the City up to 50% of the cost of purchasing
Ballistic Vests ($8961.38) for newly hired Police Officers and to replace old vests
that are either broken or have exceeded their expiration date.
The grant would cover any vests purchased after April 2022 and after the funding
for the current 2021 BJA BVP grant has been expended. Refunds will be for the
cost of body armor already submitted as part of the agency’s FY 23 Agency
Budget under “Uniforms” line item.
Fiscal Note:

Grant funding from this program would provide up to 50% reimbursement
($4,480.69) for ballistic armor purchased for sworn Henderson Police Officers.

Recommendation: Staff recommends approving the Resolution.
Attachments: Resolution 22-46
